# Japan payroll obligation inventory

Scope: payroll periods and statutory events from **1 December 2025 onward**, across all 47 prefectures, municipalities, health insurers, pension/employment-insurance classes, industries and worker categories that an employer may select. Prepared 26 September 2026. This is an **open audit inventory**, not a compliance certificate. Japan has **no JP sealed profile**, production probe or certified amount. The regional and specific-minimum-wage source registers below were copied from the shared tracker for independent work; their source-row counts do not prove a complete legal denominator.

**Product boundary.** Payroll/HRMS closure requires correct in-product selection, calculations, warnings or refusals, and saved inputs and outputs wherever the product handles an obligation. Employer actions performed outside the product remain documented with their legal trigger and owner; the product must not imply that it performed them. Lack of external-action automation alone is not an in-product calculation defect. No JP payroll or HRMS branch is closed yet.

**Pay-due input status.** The payroll run now accepts and saves a contractual pay due date separately from its settlement `pay_date`, and carries it through run and employment windows. This supplies a dated fact for future NTA table selection; no JP withholding table uses it yet. The JP profile remains unsealed until the table logic, sources and boundary probes are complete.

**Unpaid and retroactive wages remain open.** [NTA No. 2526](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2526.htm) says ordinary unpaid wages are generally withheld when actually paid, while a partial payment withholds the fraction of the full due tax corresponding to the amount actually paid; year-end adjustment includes the unpaid wage and its related tax. [NTA No. 2529](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2529.htm) gives retroactive wage differences the payment date fixed in the wage agreement, or the agreement's effective date when it fixes none, and permits distinct ordinary-wage or bonus withholding methods. The run's single optional `pay_due_date` does not capture actual payment dates/amounts, unpaid balances or different entitlement dates within a run. `mark_paid` currently records the run's derived `pay_date`, so it cannot evidence a different actual payment date. A JP table selector must receive the applicable dated wage event and payment state; until it does, the existing no-sealed-settings refusal must remain.

Each row below is a required branch group, not one test or one rate. Close a row only when the operative instrument, amendment/effective date, applicability facts, seeded rule, production calculation and adverse/boundary probes are linked. Add newly discovered subbranches before claiming a denominator. A payroll result must refuse when a required employer/employee/insurer fact is absent; it must not silently treat an unknown branch as zero.

| JP-TAX-13   | Employer-provided housing has a separate **income-tax valuation** from JP-SI-09's social-insurance prefecture table. For employees, monthly equivalent rent is based on annual building/land fixed-asset-tax bases and floor area; when the employee pays at least 50% of that calculated rent, the housing benefit is not taxable, whereas free or lower-rent housing generally creates taxable wage. Cash housing allowance or employee-direct lease reimbursement is taxable; certain mandatory on-site housing can be exempt. For officers, small housing (≤132 m² or ≤99 m² by building life), larger owned versus leased housing, and luxury housing have different rent bases; the employee 50% exemption cannot simply be applied to directors.                                                                                                                                                                                          | [NTA employee housing, current 1 April 2026](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2597.htm); [NTA officer housing, current 1 April 2026](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2600.htm); [NTA 2025 withholding guide](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/aramashi2024/)                                                                                                                                                                          | **OPEN** — occupant role, property ownership/lease, asset-tax valuation year, floor area/building life, employer and worker rent, on-site necessity and luxury assessment; verify Dec 2025 versus 2026 source continuity and probe 49.99/50% employee charge, cash allowance and small/large/luxury director housing.                                                                                  | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | No JP property-level taxable-housing valuation or role/evidence boundary probe.                                                                                                                                                    |

### Product input and mechanism blockers observed

The current `companies` model exposes one generic `region` and `risk_class`; `employment_terms` exposes broad residency, work and pay fields. The dated `employment_statutory_facts` opening record carries annual contribution bases/shares. Those inputs do not by themselves establish a Japanese worksite prefecture, specific-industry determination and individual reduction permit; employer health insurer and branch; health/pension standard-remuneration grade and determination date; April–March same-insurer standard-bonus history; or a municipality's actual June–May resident-tax notice and installment amendments. The existing opening record also does not prove a former employer's tax withholding slip. These are concrete shared-model/engine requirements, not seed values that can be assumed zero. Each must have a dated evidence path and a refusal branch for missing required facts before a JP profile can produce lawful results. Source: [Minimum Wage Act arts. 4, 6–7](https://www.japaneselawtranslation.go.jp/en/laws/download/3937/09/s34Aa001370301en14.0_h24A27.pdf), [JPS standard-remuneration decision](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20120330.html), [JPS bonus rule](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20141203.html), [NTA former-employer rule](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2674.htm), [Shinjuku municipality notice](https://www.city.shinjuku.lg.jp/hoken/file04_04_00001.html).

#### Shared implementation contract needed before a usable JP seal

1. **Workplace and wage order selection.** Record dated workplace/prefecture and industry at the person/day level where a person can move sites; retain the entity's industry classification, the worker-specific occupation/exclusion facts and any individual reduction permit with its issuing bureau, reduction percentage, dates and evidence. Extend sealed wages data from one `region → monthly/hourly amount` map to dated **overlapping** regional and specific orders, including legacy daily units, each with its own eligibility and wage-base exclusions. Select the highest applicable minimum under Minimum Wage Act art. 6 after checking the order's scope; never infer scope from the live-table `※` marker. Required probe: Iwate JPY952 → JPY1,031 on 1 December 2025; an Ibaraki worker covered by the JPY1,115 specific rate on 19 March 2026 versus its JPY1,074 regional amount, with a later higher regional rate taking precedence after its legally operative date; a second-site day in another prefecture; commuting allowance excluded; approved reduction and worker exclusion.
2. **Insurer, grade and premium history.** Model the employer's dated health insurer/branch separately from worker residence, and dated person enrolment, age, standard-remuneration decision events (acquisition, April–June regular, fixed-pay midyear), base-day counts and source files. Store each actual standard-bonus event with payment month, same-insurer key, insured status, bonus amount, standard bonus and prior-employer evidence; an annual `opening.base` alone loses the April–March and same-insurer rules. Also record the eligible establishment's JPS premium-adjustment qualifying date, election filing/start month, target-person count and any automatic stop/restart; the employee discount and employer temporary additional share/three-month offset depend on grade and **cumulative active months** (JP-SI-06). Required probe: March 2026 health-rate change versus April 2026 child-support start; care charge at the lawful 40/65 age-attainment month; September grade change, qualifying three-month/two-grade midyear change; 11/17 base-day boundary; two bonuses in one month, pension JPY1.5m cap, health JPY5.73m same-insurer cap, a transfer to a different insurer, and premium adjustment at grades JPY88,000/126,000/over 126,000 and active month 24/25/36/37, including a zero-target-person pause before the filing deadline.
3. **National and municipal tax inputs.** Keep exact official monthly/daily/bonus and annual tables as sealed, dated data; introduce a table-lookup mechanism if the CEL ladder cannot preserve every cell and selection condition. Dated employee tax declarations, dependent classifications, residency and former-employer withholding-slip verification must feed it. Store municipality-issued resident-tax notices as twelve dated installments with notice/revision identity and address municipality; never derive the installment from national withholding or last year's pay. Required probe: December 2025 year-end reform with old monthly table; January 2026 new table; 甲/乙/丙 and bonus no-prior-wage/tenfold branches; former-employer slip absent → refusal; December 2026 annual reform without applying the 2027 monthly table early; June resident-tax restart, revised notice, January–April exit lump collection and municipality change.
4. **Rounding and employer reconciliation.** Existing `round_unit`/`cents` use ordinary nearest-unit rounding; Japan needs an explicit selected method for exact 0.50 yen **down** when deducted from wages, **up** for employee cash remittance, and any proved individual agreement. For JPS health/pension office liability, sum **unrounded** person amounts then discard only the office-total fractional yen and assign employer liability as office total less summed employee deductions. Do not round each employee's employer half independently. Labour-insurance annual return similarly combines rates before truncation where wage bases coincide. Required probe: each route at .49/.50/.51 yen; two workers whose separately rounded amounts differ from a once-rounded office total; compare member charges plus employer residual to JPS notice.
5. **Dated wage and payment events.** Retain the legally applicable due date per wage/bonus or retroactive difference, plus actual paid date and amount for each full or partial payment. Select the NTA table by the applicable wage year and category, and withhold at actual payment under [NTA No. 2526](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2526.htm); preserve unpaid balances for year-end adjustment. A run-level date cannot by itself represent mixed-cadence pay events, two bonuses or a retroactive award with a distinct entitlement date. Required probe: December 2026 wages due on 31 December versus 10 January 2027; a fully unpaid wage paid later; two partial payments whose withholding fractions reconcile; and a retroactive difference with versus without an agreement-fixed payment date under [NTA No. 2529](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2529.htm).

These are shared source/model changes, so this Japan workstream does not edit them while other jurisdiction agents use the engine. After they are available, seed JP as dated sealed versions, run the focused production probes above and saved-payroll/persistence probes, and only then consider certification against the full obligation inventory.

**No seed-only JP pilot is safe yet.** `seed/seed.ts` imports every jurisdiction JSON row. A seal makes a settings version effective and immutable; it does not prove that all required Japanese schemes, wage orders and worker branches are present. An omitted scheme or unmatched contribution rule can charge zero. Even a version named for one prefecture and worker class needs a runtime boundary that refuses every unsupported worksite, insurer, employment type and pay event, together with the applicable tax, insurance, resident-tax and labour rules for that scope. The run now captures an optional pay due date, but `run/configuration.ts` still selects the sealed version at the salary period's end, and the derived `pay_date` cannot evidence a different actual payment day. A seeded version date alone cannot select or collect NTA withholding by the legally relevant wage and payment events. Until that boundary and the mechanisms above exist, the current refusal when no sealed JP version covers the payroll period is the truthful result.

The table deliberately has no compliance percentage. Its 84 branch groups and JP-RF/JP-SF source rows are **not** a final denominator until operative acts, regulations, circulars, municipal/insurer schedules and amendments are reconciled article by article. The [2026 commencement decree](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/hourei/doc/hourei/H260911T0020.pdf) resolves the JPY88,000 short-time enrolment condition's end date to 1 October 2026 even though older MHLW guidance pages still say “planned.”

Rows marked **No** include authority-facing procedural provisions: their employer applicability, source disposition and required evidence are still uncertified. A procedural obligation does not become implemented merely because it produces no payslip amount. The 2025–26 dates stated in a cited guide are source evidence; all unreviewed industry, municipality, insurer, worker and later amendment branches remain visible as open scope.

### JP-SI-02: Kyokai Kenpo 2025–26 prefecture rate register

[Kyokai Kenpo's official 2026 table](https://www.kyoukaikenpo.or.jp/about/business/insurance_rate/rate_prefectures/r08/) gives the **total** health-insurance rate for the preceding/current fiscal schedules. The 2026 column applies from **March 2026 insurance** (normally April remittance), while the new **0.23% child/childcare support charge begins with April 2026 insurance** (normally May remittance); they are separate dated rules. Care insurance adds **1.62%** for insured people aged 40–64. This register does not cover non-Kyokai insurers, grade mapping, member rounding, exemptions or bonus caps, and therefore is not a sealed contribution schedule.

### JP-EI-02: employment-insurance rate register

[Kanagawa Labour Bureau's current fiscal-year table](https://jsite.mhlw.go.jp/kanagawa-roudoukyoku/hourei_seido_tetsuzuki/roudou_hoken/hourei_seido/2024_00008.html) gives employee/employer shares in **per thousand of eligible wages**. The 2025 fiscal schedule governs 1 December 2025–31 March 2026; the 2026 fiscal schedule begins 1 April 2026. This table does not decide membership, wage-base eligibility or the different annual-return rounding in JP-EI-03.

### JP-TAX-07/08: 1 April 2026 taxable-benefit transitions

The [NTA commuting schedule](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2026tsukin/index.htm) applies to allowances **payable** from 1 April 2026, except a later top-up of an allowance payable earlier. One-way distance must be measured for the transport-tool segment, and employer-paid qualifying parking adds actual cost up to JPY5,000/month; mixed public transport still has the JPY150,000 overall cap. These are employee-income-tax exemptions, not exclusions from the employment-insurance wage base or minimum-wage comparison.

| One-way transport-tool distance | April 2026 monthly exemption | Official source                                                           | Required profile/probe                  | Implemented?              | If not, why?                                  |
| ------------------------------- | ---------------------------: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| Under 2 km                      |                         JPY0 | [NTA 2026 table](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2026tsukin/index.htm) | Exact 2-km boundary, parking ineligible | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | No JP vehicle/parking taxability calculation. |
| 2 to under 10 km                |                     JPY4,200 | [NTA 2026 table](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2026tsukin/index.htm) | 2/10-km boundary, qualifying parking    | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | No JP vehicle/parking taxability calculation. |

The [NTA meal notice](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2026shokuji/index.htm) separately amended Basic Circular 36-38-2 on 31 March 2026: for meals provided from 1 April, the worker must actually pay **at least 50%** of value and the employer-funded monthly balance must be **at most JPY7,500**. The pre-April balance cap was JPY3,500. The distinct substitute-cash ceiling for qualifying late-night meals rose from JPY300 to JPY650 per occasion. Include actual collection and date rather than assuming a contracted meal plan meets the test.

### JP-SI-09: 2026 prefectural in-kind remuneration schedule

The [JPS 2026 schedule](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20150511.files/2026.pdf) values meals from **1 April 2026**; its housing column through **30 September 2026** uses living-room **tatami mats**, and its column from **1 October 2026** uses **total square metres**. Each value is JPY per person, except the housing unit. Do not use NTA's separate tax-exempt meal ceiling for this social-insurance valuation. Select the applicable worksite/managed branch, with a separate dispatching-agency rule and possible health-society exception. The two tables are **source transcriptions only**, not sealed or exercised. The 2025/2026 values still require authoritative instrument and branch reconciliation before they can be used for payroll.

#### December 2025–March 2026 predecessor schedule

The [JPS 2025 schedule](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20150511.files/2025.pdf) is effective from 1 April 2025 and remains the meal valuation for December 2025–March 2026. Its housing values remain the tatami-basis values through 30 September 2026 under the successor schedule. This is a transcription of the source and does not validate use of the value in production.

### JP-SI-06: elected short-time-worker premium adjustment

[JPS's September 2026 eligibility notice](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hokenryochosei/jigyosho.html) limits the election to specified employer entry routes. These include an establishment voluntarily becoming a specified applicable establishment from 1 October 2026 through 30 September 2035 and establishments newly becoming specified under the phased employer-size expansion: 36–50 ordinary pension members from October 2027, 21–35 from October 2029, 11–20 from October 2032 and ten or fewer from October 2035. Certain individual establishments joining or reaching specified status have separate routes. The employer must file by the end of the month before the month containing the second anniversary of qualification; an October 2026 voluntary qualification therefore permits filing through **30 September 2028**. An establishment already specified on 1 October 2026, or one that had at least 51 members before one of these routes, is excluded. The first qualifying route sets the deadline when routes overlap.

[JPS's worker rule](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hokenryochosei/hihokensha.html) applies the elected scheme to **every** eligible short-time insured worker at that establishment with standard remuneration of JPY126,000 or less, not a selected subset. Short-time status requires less than three quarters of ordinary weekly hours or monthly working days, contracted hours of at least 20 a week and no student exclusion under the specified-establishment route. A worker insured at multiple establishments is excluded, as is one whose health/pension premium is exempt during qualifying prenatal/postnatal or childcare leave. A voluntary pension member aged 70 or more can receive health-premium adjustment only, where covered. The 1 October 2026 abolition of the JPY88,000 wage condition is confirmed by the commencement decree in JP-SI-01; JPS's worker guidance still calls it planned.

[JPS's exact calculation](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hokenryochosei/keisan.html) uses `standard monthly remuneration × applicable health or pension total rate × reduction percentage`; round a fractional-yen **reduction amount** to the nearest yen with 0.5 rounded up before subtracting it from the normal employee share. The reduction percentages for standard monthly remuneration JPY88,000/98,000/104,000/110,000/118,000/126,000 are respectively **25/20/14/9/5/2%** for active months 1–24 and **12.5/10/7/4.5/2.5/1%** for months 25–36. Bonus, care and child-support charges are excluded from the reduction. This distinct half-up rule for the reduction amount must not overwrite JP-SI-05's default half-down rule for employee payroll deduction.

[JPS's term and suspension guidance](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hokenryochosei/gaiyo.html) starts the three-year clock in the election filing month and bars a voluntary early stop. If the eligible-worker count becomes zero **before the election deadline**, the scheme automatically stops and inactive months do not consume the 36-month term; restart requires a timely employer application and cannot be retroactive. If the count first becomes zero **after the deadline**, no automatic stop occurs and the clock continues. [JPS's offset guidance](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hokenryochosei/kanpu.html) credits the employer's temporary extra payment against liability three months later, but can deny the offset for that month after failure to pay by both the normal and demand deadlines. Required probes: eligible and excluded establishments/workers; 88k/98k/126k/over-126k grades; a fractional reduction of exactly JPY0.50; active months 24/25/36/37; a zero-worker stop before versus after the filing deadline and timely versus late restart; unpaid premium withholding the employer offset. No JP profile or production probe yet exists.

### JP-HR-05/12: 2025 childcare and family-care amendment branches

The following are individually traceable duties already effective at the requested December 2025 start; the underlying [MHLW childcare](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/seisakunitsuite/bunya/koyou_roudou/koyoukintou/ryouritsu/ikuji/law-amendment/) and [family-care](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/seisakunitsuite/bunya/koyou_roudou/koyoukintou/ryouritsu/kaigo/law-amendment/) guides distinguish duties from recommended measures. They do not establish that every leave day is employer-paid. Each row needs a saved employee/worksite case and evidence of the nonpayroll action where applicable.

| ID / commencement    | Worker or employer selection, rule and official source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Required mechanism and probe                                                                                                | Implemented?              | If not, why?                                                          |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |

## Existing source register and open findings

**Open; missing jurisdiction coverage.** No JP public law profile is present. First-pass
Labour Standards groups and a 42-article Minimum Wage Act/18-article Enforcement Regulation
crosswalk are recorded below, but neither is a reconciled branch-complete denominator or an
implemented amount. Every active
MY-nihon settings row has `jurisdiction_code: "MY"`; its Japanese-associated name supplies no
Japanese tax, social insurance, employment or reporting rules. JP needs the same complete twelve
requirement families from 1 December 2025, with actual local and insurer inputs.

Initial official source review establishes a specific commencement distinction:

- [NTA's 2025 basic/salary/dependent deduction reform page](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2025kiso/)
  was read. The reform generally commences **1 December 2025** for 2025-and-later income tax;
  December year-end adjustment uses revised annual deductions. Monthly/daily withholding uses
  the revised tables for payments due from **January 2026**. The dependent/specified-relative
  declaration and annual-income thresholds are not interchangeable with a simple child count.
- [NTA's 2025 year-end guide](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/nencho2025/01.htm)
  and [current withholding index](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/) were located. Complete
  form/table review remains open; locating these sources is not a full legal read.
- [NTA's 2026 reform notice](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2026kiso/index.htm),
  [April 2026 amendment guide](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/2026kaisei.pdf),
  [2026 year-end guide](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/nencho2026/pdf/102.pdf)
  and [implementation Q&A](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2026kiso/pdf/0026005-024.pdf)
  were read. The 2026 basic deduction, salary-income deduction and family-income tests generally
  commence **1 December 2026** and apply to 2026 annual income. January–November 2026 payroll
  withholding remains on the [2026 monthly/daily/bonus tables](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/zeigakuhyo2026/01.htm);
  December year-end adjustment recomputes the annual tax under the new deductions and reconciles
  earlier withholding. A relative newly qualifying under the revised income tests enters
  withholding on wages due from 1 December only after the revised declaration. The new periodic
  withholding tables begin **1 January 2027**, not 1 December 2026. Someone whose final 2026
  resident wage and adjustment occurred before 1 December needs a tax return or correction to
  benefit from the new annual deductions; rerunning that earlier payroll under December law is
  incorrect.
- The [published 2027 withholding tables](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/zeigakuhyo2027/01.htm)
  explicitly replace the 2026 tables for 2027 wages. Under
  [NTA's 2027 surtax Q&A](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/pdf/0026005-024_03.pdf),
  defense income tax starts at 1% of the underlying income tax and reconstruction income tax
  falls from 2.1% to 1.1% for income arising from 1 January 2027. The combined uplift remains
  2.1%, so unchanged aggregate withholding cannot be used as evidence that the component,
  effective-date and reporting treatment is correct. The relevant salary/bonus due date controls
  the boundary; the Q&A lists exceptions for certain director bonuses and retroactive pay.
- [NTA's basic-deduction table](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/shotoku/1199.htm)
  sets the 2026–2027 resident deduction at JPY1.04m for total income up to JPY1.32m, then
  JPY880k/680k/670k/620k through the successive JPY3.36m/4.89m/6.55m/23.5m bounds. The
  extra resident amounts do not apply unchanged to nonresidents. The
  [salary-income guidance](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/shotoku/1410.htm)
  raises the 2026–2027 minimum salary deduction to JPY740k, with special income-result bands
  at JPY691k–741k and JPY2.191m–2.2m annual salary; below JPY6.6m the statutory year-end
  table controls. The [2026 amendment guide](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/2026kaisei.pdf)
  raises the eligible dependent/same-livelihood spouse income ceiling from JPY580k to JPY620k,
  with salary-only equivalents JPY1.23m to JPY1.36m. Its separate specified-relative,
  spouse-special and working-student bands must also be modelled. The
  [2026 year-end guide](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/nencho2026/pdf/102.pdf)
  adds a 2026–2027 general life-insurance deduction branch for a dependent under 23: qualifying
  new-policy premiums yield up to JPY60k instead of JPY40k, while the combined life/medical/
  pension insurance deduction cap remains JPY120k. The premium bands, old/new-policy combination
  and dependent eligibility need independent cases.
- [NTA table-selection guidance](https://www.nta.go.jp/taxes/shiraberu/taxanswer/gensen/2511.htm)
  distinguishes monthly, daily and bonus tables, 甲/乙 declarations and qualifying daily-worker
  丙 treatment. Bonus withholding uses a separate table except for no prior-month wage or a
  bonus above ten times prior-month wage, when the monthly-table method applies. Tax-table input
  is pay after specified employee social-insurance deductions. These are required inputs and
  selection branches, not one general progressive tax formula.
- The [MHLW 2025 regional minimum-wage table](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/content/11200000/001571192.pdf)
  was read for all 47 prefectures. Its effective dates cross the requested period: Iwate is
  JPY952 before **1 December 2025** and JPY1,031 from that date; Yamagata is JPY955 before
  **23 December 2025** and JPY1,032 from that date; Fukushima is JPY955 before
  **1 January 2026** and JPY1,033 from that date; Akita is JPY951 before **31 March 2026**
  and JPY1,031 from that date. The same table also crosses the support period in Gunma
  (JPY985 → JPY1,063 on **1 March 2026**), Tokushima (JPY980 → JPY1,046 on
  **1 January 2026**), Oita (JPY954 → JPY1,035 on **1 January 2026**) and Kumamoto
  (JPY952 → JPY1,034 on **1 January 2026**). Tokyo's JPY1,226 rate began 3 October 2025.
  These are dated
  regional floors, not a single nationwide rate. [MHLW's wage-base guidance](https://saiteichingin.mhlw.go.jp/point/page_point_targetwages.html)
  excludes bonuses, overtime/holiday/night premium, commuting, family and attendance allowances
  from the floor comparison. Applicable [industry-specific floors](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/stf/seisakunitsuite/bunya/0000041788.html)
  also need worker/industry selection. The [2026 prefecture response table](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/content/11200000/001745586.pdf)
  records council recommendations and planned commencement dates, while the ministry's
  [live national quick table](https://saiteichingin.mhlw.go.jp/table/page_list_nationallist.php)
  now displays a 2026 successor amount and date for all 47 prefectures. Neither source alone
  establishes Gazette publication for every row. [MHLW's 3 September 2026 release](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/stf/newpage_75950.html)
  says all 47 successor amounts were council **recommendations**, planned to commence between
  **1 October and 2 December 2026** after objection and prefectural-director decision; none
  replaces a 2025-order amount for work through 26 September 2026. [Tokyo Labour Bureau](https://jsite.mhlw.go.jp/tokyo-hellowork/list/kiba/minimum-wage-information_2026.html)
  confirms Tokyo's **JPY1,280/hour** determination was promulgated on **1 September 2026**
  for **1 October 2026**; [Hokkaido Labour Bureau](https://jsite.mhlw.go.jp/hokkaido-roudoukyoku/minimum_wage.html)
  likewise confirms its **JPY1,131/hour** determination was promulgated on 1 September for
  1 October. [Miyagi Labour Bureau](https://jsite.mhlw.go.jp/miyagi-roudoukyoku/content/contents/002799063.pdf)
  confirms its **JPY1,098/hour** determination was promulgated on 1 September for 1 October.
  Osaka, Kanagawa, Kyoto and Nara also have issuer-confirmed 2026 decisions
  in JP-RF14/24/25/29 below; the other 40 require individual final-publication checks,
  even if some have already been promulgated. Full prefecture/sector instrument review and production selection
  remain open.
- The [NTA 2025 year-end calculation guide, pp. 35–39](https://www.nta.go.jp/publication/pamph/gensen/nencho2025/pdf/109.pdf)
  was read. It requires aggregation of 2025 wages and prior withholding, qualifying unpaid
  wages and taxable benefits in kind. A midyear hire's qualifying former-employer wages and tax
  enter the adjustment only after verification from the former employer's withholding slip;
  without that evidence, defer the adjustment. The 2025 salary deduction table is distinct from
  2024's; taxable annual salary is floored to JPY1,000 before the annual tax table, reconstruction
  tax multiplies the adjusted income tax by **102.1%**, and the resulting annual tax is floored
  to JPY100 before the over/under-withholding settlement. The final wage's normal withholding
  may be calculated or omitted as the guide describes; that choice must not double-count it.
- The [NTA's November 2025 commuter-allowance notice](https://www.nta.go.jp/users/gensen/2025tsukin/index.htm)
  was read. Revised tax-exempt monthly automobile/bicycle limits took effect 20 November 2025
  but apply to qualifying allowances due from **1 April 2025**. For one-way distance at least
  55 km, the limit rose from JPY31,600 to **JPY38,700**; for 45–under-55 km, from JPY28,000
  to **JPY32,300**. Other distance bands and mixed transport have separate limits. December
  adjustment must be able to correct earlier-2025 taxable commuting pay; it must not treat
  the 20 November promulgation or 1 December support start as the applicable-income boundary.
- [Japan Pension Service's qualification-time decision](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20120330.html)
  sets standard monthly remuneration from remuneration at the new employment under its
  prescribed estimation methods, with
  the employer filing the acquisition notice within five days. An acquisition from 1 June to
  31 December normally keeps that decision through the next August. The [2026 regular-decision
  guidance](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20121017.html) uses eligible
  April–June remuneration and applies the revised grade from September through the next August;
  the employer files by 10 July, subject to listed acquisition/change exclusions. Standard
  monthly remuneration is **not** each month's actual gross or a former employer's salary.
  [Midyear revision guidance](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20150515-02.html)
  requires a fixed-pay change followed by three eligible remuneration months and generally a
  two-grade difference, effective in the fourth month, with specified one-grade ceiling/floor
  exceptions and direction-of-change tests. Part-time base-day thresholds differ: the regular
  calculation uses at least 17 days, and covered short-time workers in specified workplaces
  use at least 11. These mechanism rules are read; insurer-specific rates, complete grade
  tables, exceptions and source-to-payroll execution remain open.
- [JPS pension contribution guidance](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20150515-01.html)
  uses the determined monthly grade or standard bonus, with an 18.3% pension rate split equally.
  Regular remuneration includes recurring allowances and in-kind value; payments made at least
  four times a year enter monthly remuneration. The
  [JPS bonus procedure](https://www.nenkin.go.jp/service/kounen/hokenryo/hoshu/20141203.html)
  aggregates multiple payments in one month before flooring gross bonus to JPY1,000, and
  caps the pension standard bonus at JPY1.5m per month. Health-insurance standard bonuses instead
  have a **JPY5.73m April–March fiscal-year cap**. For a job/transfer change within that year,
  prior standard bonus amounts under the **same insurer** still count and a cumulative-amount
  declaration may be required; amounts under a different insurer do not enter that insurer's
  ceiling. Premium liability in the month of loss of insured status differs between pension and
  health insurance; both can still contribute to the health-insurance cumulative standard-bonus
  record. A generic prior-salary field cannot implement these rules. The filing deadline is five
  days after the bonus payment.
- [Kyokai Kenpo's dated rate schedule](https://www.kyoukaikenpo.or.jp/about/business/insurance_rate/rate_prefectures/)
  makes health rates prefecture- and year-specific, and its [2026 schedule](https://www.kyoukaikenpo.or.jp/lp/2026hokenryou/)
  states a 1.62% care rate for covered ages and a separate 0.23% child/childcare support rate.
  The [Children and Families Agency](https://www.cfa.go.jp/policies/kodomokosodateshienkinseido)
  confirms the new support contribution begins with **April 2026 insurance** (normally deducted
  from May pay) and is shared equally by employer and employee. The actual employer's insurer
  branch and the month of insurance liability, rather than employee residence or payslip month
  alone, select the rate. Complete 2025/2026 insurer tables and age, enrolment, bonus and
  collection timing remain open.

**Regional floor source register, extracted 26 September 2026.** The ministry's
[live national quick table](https://saiteichingin.mhlw.go.jp/table/page_list_nationallist.php)
shows the 47 listed predecessor/successor pairs below. The first rate column becomes operative
on its stated date; for Yamagata, Fukushima, Akita, Gunma, Tokushima, Oita and Kumamoto, the
[2025 ministry table](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/content/11200000/001571192.pdf) supplies the
older amount that still governs part of the requested period, as itemised above. The 2026
column is a **source candidate**, not a sealed legal rate: final Gazette publication and
industry/worker coverage must be checked per prefecture. “Bureau-confirmed” means the
linked issuing labour bureau statement corroborates a final decision. All 47
selection paths and boundary probes remain open; this is a rate-source register, not a
production rule.

**Specific-floor instrument register is still open.** [MHLW reports 223 specific
minimum wages as of 1 April 2026](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/stf/seisakunitsuite/bunya/0000041788.html),
with 112 amended during FY2025, and publishes a separate
[prefecture/industry list](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/stf/seisakunitsuite/bunya/koyou_roudou/roudoukijun/chingin/newpage_43846.html).
Each instrument's industry, occupation, excluded workers, amount, unit and operative dates
still needs an ID and a regional-floor comparison; the 223 is an as-of date instrument
count, not a payroll-branch denominator. The same ministry list retains the nationwide
nonmetallic-mining determination at **JPY5,772 per day** from 17 May 1989, with worker and
wage-component exceptions. [2007 Amendment Act supplemental Art. 5(2)](https://www.shugiin.go.jp/internet/itdb_housei.nsf/html/housei/16820071205129.htm)
and the [2008 implementing circular](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/web/t_doc?dataId=00tb5425&dataType=1&pageNo=1)
explain why an older specific determination can retain a nonhourly unit until amended or
abolished. A general hourly-only selector would misclassify this instrument. Its present
amount may be overtaken by the applicable regional floor, but that comparison needs the
worker's location, scheduled/actual hours and instrument scope; do not assume the legacy
determination has disappeared.

**Dated count reconciliation, 26 September 2026.** The ministry's
[1 April 2026 count](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/content/11200000/001665408.pdf) breaks the
223 determinations into **222 prefectural and one nationwide**. Its
[live prefectural industry table](https://saiteichingin.mhlw.go.jp/table/page_indlist_nationallist.html)
still has **223 rows**: it includes the old Nara wood/furniture determination, JP-SF149.
The [15 January 2026 table](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/content/11200000/001638874.pdf)
likewise includes it because it preceded abolition. On 2 March 2026 the
[Nara labour council recommended abolition](https://jsite.mhlw.go.jp/nara-roudoukyoku/content/contents/002629442.pdf)
effective the day before Gazette publication; the
[Nara bureau's notice](https://jsite.mhlw.go.jp/nara-roudoukyoku/newpage_01053.html)
scheduled publication for 1 April 2026 absent objection. The ministry's 1 April count
and [Nara bureau's current three-industry list](https://jsite.mhlw.go.jp/nara-roudoukyoku/hourei_seido_tetsuzuki/tingin/hourei_seido/saiteitingin008.html)
corroborate that the Nara wood/furniture order ceased by then, which reconciles the
one-row difference: **223 prefectural rows in the stale live table − one Nara abolition =
222 prefectural operative determinations**, plus the nationwide nonmetallic-mining order.
The exact Gazette abolition publication remains to be linked; retain the older Nara
instrument for December 2025–March 2026 boundary review, not current selection.
Five prefecture headers (Gifu, Kyoto, Tokushima, Kumamoto and Okinawa) occupy separate
HTML table rows; their following industry rows are included in the count. A rate/date
multiset comparison of the 223 live rows with the 223 January PDF rows matched 222;
the sole mismatch was Fukui's effective year in JP-SF-T05. The live table omits many
worker exclusions and cannot itself establish Gazette effectivity.
The [April statistical PDF](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/content/11200000/001665408.pdf)
has a separate subtotal typo: its nationwide old-industry section displays **one**
determination, labels that section's subtotal **two**, yet its grand total of **223**
uses 222 prefectural plus **one** nationwide. The reconciled count does not close each
instrument's scope, dates and comparison mechanism.

The first three daily amounts appear in the [ministry's live table](https://saiteichingin.mhlw.go.jp/table/page_indlist_nationallist.html);
the nationwide amount appears in the [ministry's determination list](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/stf/seisakunitsuite/bunya/koyou_roudou/roudoukijun/chingin/newpage_43846.html).
All four remain **OPEN**; a `※` on the hourly figure alone is not a disposition of the
legacy daily instrument.

**Labour Standards Act provision pass, 26 September 2026.** The [MHLW consolidated
Act](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/web/t_doc?dataId=73022000) and [e-Gov law index](https://laws.e-gov.go.jp/law/322AC0000000049)
were read for the provisions below. These are **first-pass groups**, not a final article-by-article
denominator: each listed paragraph, exception, delegated Cabinet/ministerial rule, current
amendment and sector-specific provision still needs an independent disposition. The Act's
payroll amount, scheduling and employer evidence paths are absent from the JP profile.

| Obligation                                            | Act provision and source-backed branch                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Required mechanism, evidence and disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Implemented?              | If not, why?                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| JP-LS01 — scope and average wage                      | [Arts. 9–12][jp-lsa] define worker, employer, wage and the prior-three-month average wage. Art. 12 uses a calendar-day denominator, a worked-day minimum for time/piece rates, wage-cutoff anchoring, excluded periods and wage types, and a short-service branch.                                                                                                                                                          | Capture actual wage history, workdays, cutoff, leave/closure periods and pay components. Independently price a shutdown allowance and dismissal-notice pay with monthly versus hourly/piece pay. **OPEN:** no JP average-wage path; reconcile the ministry's fallback determinations and rounding. | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS02 — notice and exit settlement                  | [Arts. 19–23][jp-lsa] distinguish dismissal restrictions, 30 days' notice or average-wage substitute, shortened notice after paid days, and worker categories whose exemption ends after specified continued service. Requested certificates and payment/return of earned wages and property within seven days of a qualifying request after death or exit are separate; disputed sums do not defer the undisputed portion. | Capture reason, notice and paid notice days, exemption category/continued service, request date, disputed portion and actual disbursement. Probe 29/30 notice days and a requested exit payment with one disputed component. **OPEN.**                                                             | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS03 — ordinary wage payment and lawful deductions | [Art. 24][jp-lsa] requires full direct payment in money at least once each month on a fixed date, with statutory/collective exceptions and a majority-worker written agreement for other deductions; irregular wages and bonuses have the stated timing exception.                                                                                                                                                          | Select the actual legal deduction basis and agreement, due date and payment method; refuse an unsupported recovery. Verify paid status, not only a computed payslip. **OPEN.**                                                                                                                     | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS04 — emergency, shutdown and piecework wages     | [Arts. 25–28][jp-lsa] require early payment of already-earned wages on a qualifying emergency request, at least 60% of Art. 12 average wage for employer-caused closure, and a work-time-based wage guarantee for piece/task work. Art. 28 refers minimum-wage amounts to the Minimum Wage Act.                                                                                                                             | Capture emergency request, employer-responsibility finding, closure days, output/time and prefecture/industry floor. Probe each branch and interaction with payroll timing. **OPEN.**                                                                                                              | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS05 — hours, rest and agreement limits            | [Arts. 32–36, 40][jp-lsa] set normal weekly/daily hours, variable-hour/flexible schedules, rest and weekly/four-week holiday rules, and filed agreements for overtime or holiday work. Art. 36 includes ordinary monthly/yearly limits and conditional special limits with rolling averages; Arts. 33 and 40 have emergency/sector routes.                                                                                  | Capture governing schedule/period, filed agreement and dates, actual hours, statutory holidays, exception approval and rolling history. Price work and separately warn/refuse unlawful schedules. Split each schedule and sector arm before closure. **OPEN.**                                     | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS06 — premium pay and deemed hours                | [Arts. 37–38-4][jp-lsa] price overtime, statutory-holiday and ordinarily 22:00–05:00 night work from an eligible wage base, with an above-60-hours monthly tier and a conditional paid-leave substitution. The later articles govern aggregation and specified deemed-hour arrangements.                                                                                                                                    | Distinguish statutory from contractual overtime/holidays, overlapping night hours, excluded allowances, agreement and actual/deemed hours. Reconcile Cabinet premium rates and ministerial wage-base exclusions, then probe threshold and overlap cases. **OPEN.**                                 | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS07 — paid annual leave                           | [Art. 39][jp-lsa] requires six months' service and 80% attendance for initial leave, service-based increments, a short-week/annual-days table, a conditional five-day hourly allotment, worker-requested timing and employer-set five-day usage where entitlement reaches ten days. Leave pay follows the workplace's lawful average/ordinary/insured-grade method; specified absence counts as attendance.                 | Capture continuous service, scheduled/actual days, deemed attendance, agreement, grant/use dates and chosen wage basis. Probe 79.99/80% attendance, part-time entitlement, hourly limit, five-day usage and all pay methods. **OPEN.**                                                             | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS08 — hour and premium exclusions                 | [Arts. 40–41-2][jp-lsa] condition exceptions for certain sectors, managers/confidential staff, authorised intermittent work and consenting high-level professionals. The exemption's actual terms and approvals matter; a job title alone does not switch off wage law.                                                                                                                                                     | Capture category, agency permission or committee decision, written consent and conditions; verify which hours, rest, holiday or night-premium rules remain. **OPEN.**                                                                                                                              | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS09 — protected leave and work limits             | [Arts. 65–68][jp-lsa] cover prenatal request, postnatal work ban/limited return, requested pregnancy restrictions, nursing time and requested menstrual leave. These provisions do not themselves establish that every such hour is paid.                                                                                                                                                                                   | Capture birth/medical/request dates, actual work and the separate contractual/statutory pay or insurance-benefit source. Probe work prohibition and pay classification separately. **OPEN.**                                                                                                       | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS10 — workplace injury compensation               | [Arts. 75–83][jp-lsa] cover medical expense, 60%-of-average-wage absence compensation and later adjustments, disability/death and other remedies. Insurer payments/coverage require separate reconciliation before offsetting the employer's duty.                                                                                                                                                                          | Capture injury classification, certified absence, average-wage history, assessment and insurer determination; distinguish employer payable from insurer benefit. **OPEN.**                                                                                                                         | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS11 — work rules and records                      | [Arts. 89–92, 106–109, 143][jp-lsa] require a filed work-rules instrument for establishments with at least ten regular workers, including wage, hours, leave and termination terms; communication, worker register, wage ledger entries at each payment and record retention. Art. 143 presently substitutes **three years** for Art. 109's printed five-year period.                                                       | Capture establishment headcount, filed/effective work-rules version, communication evidence, payment-by-payment ledger fields and retention start. Reconcile the Enforcement Ordinance and the Art. 143 transition before an export or deletion policy is certified. **OPEN.**                     | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-LS12 — statutory scope exclusions and remedies     | [Arts. 114–116, 143][jp-lsa] cover court-ordered additional payment, claim limitation and exclusions including seafarers, family-only businesses and domestic servants. Art. 143 also modifies the stated limitation periods during its transition.                                                                                                                                                                         | Classify the actual worker/employer and preserve claim/notice/order evidence; do not treat a court remedy as routine payroll or a job label as an exclusion. **OPEN.**                                                                                                                             | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |

[jp-lsa]: https://www.mhlw.go.jp/web/t_doc?dataId=73022000

**Reconciliation still required:** this pass does not dispose of every article of the Act,
its Enforcement Ordinance, the Minimum Wage Act, the 2025–2026 amendment/commencement history,
or all special worker/industry rules. The next pass must split these grouped branches, check
each cross-reference against its delegated rule and record a sourced disposition for every
otherwise unlisted provision. No row here has a JP saved-input → payroll-output probe.

**Minimum Wage Act provision pass, 26 September 2026.** The [MHLW consolidated
Act](https://www.mhlw.go.jp/web/t_doc?dataId=73053000&dataType=0), [e-Gov revision
index](https://laws.e-gov.go.jp/law/334AC0000000137) and [MHLW comparison
method](https://saiteichingin.mhlw.go.jp/point/page_point_check.html) establish these
branches. The 47 prefectural and applicable industry determinations and their operative dates
still need a complete independent read. The Act and Enforcement Regulation article crosswalks
below are provisional; paragraph-level, amendment and branch reconciliation remains open.

| Obligation                                | Provision and source-backed branch                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Required mechanism and disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Implemented?              | If not, why?                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| JP-MW01 — worker scope and rate unit      | [Arts. 2–4][jp-mwa] define the covered worker and ordinarily an **hourly** minimum. The 2007 amendment's transitional Art. 5(2) can preserve a pre-2008 specific determination in another unit until its first effective amendment/abolition. A below-floor contract term is ineffective and replaced by the minimum. Family-only businesses and domestic servants are outside the Act's worker definition. | Select actual worker/engagement and instrument vintage; compare the lawful hourly-equivalent wage with the dated regional floor and any separately applicable legacy specific floor. Probe monthly, daily, hourly and piece-rate workers, including the nationwide daily mining determination below. **OPEN.** | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-MW02 — eligible wage basis             | [Arts. 4(3)–(4), 5][jp-mwa] exclude specified nonmonthly and nonordinary pay and require proper valuation of in-kind pay or employer-supplied items; an authorised nonwork period is not automatically an hourly floor shortfall. [MHLW's wage guide][jp-minwage-basis] lists bonuses, overtime/holiday/night premiums and commuting/family/attendance allowances outside the comparison.                   | Classify each pay line, normal hours and permitted unpaid hours; do not let a bonus or overtime cure deficient base pay. Verify any in-kind valuation under the delegated rule. **OPEN.**                                                                                                                      | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-MW03 — multiple floors and location    | [Arts. 6, 9–19][jp-mwa] require the highest applicable regional/specific floor; Art. 13 takes a dispatched worker's regional floor from the client workplace, and Art. 18 applies the client's relevant specific floor. The promulgated rate's effective date governs; a proposed rate is not law.                                                                                                          | Capture worksite prefecture, dispatch/client location, industry/occupation and dated determinations. Probe two simultaneous floors, a dispatched worker, the Iwate/Yamagata/Akita effective-date edges and Tokyo's promulgated JPY1,226 → JPY1,280 boundary on 1 October 2026. **OPEN.**                       | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-MW04 — individual reduction permission | [Art. 7][jp-mwa] permits a reduced floor for listed worker categories only after prefectural labour-bureau permission and a determined reduction; probation, disability, training or light work alone is insufficient.                                                                                                                                                                                      | Preserve individual permit, covered floor, approved percentage, effective period and category. Probe the same probationer with and without a valid permit. **OPEN.**                                                                                                                                           | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |
| JP-MW05 — notice and enforcement evidence | [Art. 8][jp-mwa] requires employers to make the applicable floor known at the workplace. Arts. 27–42 include official inspection, reporting and penalty provisions; Arts. 10–12 and 15–26 contain actor-specific determination and council procedures, while the resulting rates still govern payroll.                                                                                                      | Keep current workplace notice, source determination and inspection/response evidence; separate a wage shortfall payable to the worker from an employer penalty. Reconcile each administrative article before marking it out of payroll scope. **OPEN.**                                                        | No [IN-SYSTEM 2026-09-29] | Source exceptions and required inputs remain open; no JP sealed rule or production probe. |

**Minimum Wage Act article crosswalk, 26 September 2026.** This [consolidated Act][jp-mwa]
has 42 operative articles. Every article below has a provisional disposition; **all 42 remain
OPEN** until its paragraphs, delegated rules, amendments and dated local determinations are
reconciled. “Authority” means the provision primarily directs a public body, not that the
resulting rate or an employer response can be ignored. This pass exposes three branches missing
from the five grouped rows above: Art. 15(4)'s time-limited industry exception, Art. 34(2)'s
anti-retaliation duty, and Art. 35's distinct seafarer floor. No claim of whole-instrument
closure follows from accounting for its article numbers.

**Minimum Wage Act Enforcement Regulation crosswalk, 26 September 2026.** The
[MHLW consolidated regulation][jp-mwa-reg] supplies the operative conversion and reduction
rules delegated by the Act. Its 18 principal articles are accounted for below; **all remain
OPEN** pending paragraph-level branch probes, amendment and local-instrument reconciliation.
The [e-Gov revision index](https://laws.e-gov.go.jp/law/334M50002000016) identifies the
regulation and its effective versions. Most notably, using `monthly pay ÷ 30 ÷ 8` as a
universal hourly comparator would conflict with Regulation Art. 2.
